While significant research focuses on mitigating harmful content generation, the enhanced safety often come with the side effect of over-refusal, where LLMs may reject innocuous prompts and become less helpful. Although the issue of over-refusal has been empirically observed, a systematic measurement is challenging due to the difficulty of crafting prompts that can elicit the over-refusal behaviors of LLMs. This study proposes a novel method for automatically generating large-scale over-refusal datasets. Leveraging this technique, we introduce OR-Bench, the first large-scale over-refusal benchmark. OR-Bench comprises 80,000 over-refusal prompts across 10 common rejection categories, a subset of around 1,000 hard prompts that are challenging even for state-of-the-art LLMs, and an additional 600 toxic prompts to prevent indiscriminate responses. We then conduct a comprehensive study to measure the over-refusal of 32 popular LLMs across 8 model families. Our datasets are publicly available at [this https URL](https://huggingface.co/bench-llms) and our codebase is open-sourced at [this https URL](https://github.com/justincui03/or-bench). We hope this benchmark can help the community develop better safety aligned models. \n\nComments: | Accepted to ICML 2025, we thank everyone for their valuable suggestions and feedback!  \n---|--- Consider activating javascript to get access to all site functionality. \n\n## [AI ALIGNMENT FORUM](/)\n\n## [AF](/)\n\nLogin\n\nA Sober Look at Steering Vectors for LLMs — AI Alignment Forum\n\n[Activation Engineering](/w/activation-engineering)[Interpretability (ML & AI)](/w/interpretability-ml-and-ai)[AI](/w/ai)\n\nFrontpage\n\n# 17\n\n# [A Sober Look at Steering Vectors for LLMs](/posts/QQP4nq7TXg89CJGBh/a-sober-look-at-steering-vectors-for-llms)\n\nby [Joschka Braun](/users/joschka-braun?from=post_header), [Dmitrii Krasheninnikov](/users/dmitrii-krasheninnikov?from=post_header), [Usman Anwar](/users/usman-anwar?from=post_header), [RobertKirk](/users/robertkirk?from=post_header), [Daniel Tan](/users/daniel-tan?from=post_header), [David Scott Krueger](/users/david-scott-krueger?from=post_header)\n\n23rd Nov 2024\n\n6 min read\n\n0\n\n# 17\n\n _We thank Madeline Brumley, Joe Kwon, David Chanin and Itamar Pres for their helpful feedback._\n\n##  Introduction\n\nControlling LLM behavior through directly intervening on internal activations is an appealing idea. [Various](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.06681) [methods](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.01405) [for](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.15213) [controlling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.15916) [LLM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.03341) [behavior](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.06668) through activation steering have been proposed. Most steering methods add a 'steering vector' (SV) to the model’s activations at a given layer and token position during inference. This approach leverages the hypothesis that many human-interpretable 'concepts' like [truthfulness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.06824), [refusal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2406.11717), and [sentiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.15154) are represented as directions in activation space. Steering interventions are appealing because they use much less data than fine-tuning and do not require changes to the model parameters. In principle, this makes them more efficient and easy to controlling properties of the generated text in a desired way.\n\nHowever, steering methods face a variety of significant challenges that hinder their practical applicability, comparability and further improvement. In this blog post, we discuss recent work performed at [KASL](https://www.kasl.ai/) and [UCL DARK](https://ucldark.com/) investigating challenges to steering methods for LLMs, focusing on 3 key challenges:\n\n  1. [**Current steering methods have substantial limitations**](/posts/QQP4nq7TXg89CJGBh/a-sober-look-at-current-steering-methods-for-llms#Current_steering_methods_have_substantial_limitations)\n     1. When evaluated more thoroughly or in different settings, many steering methods turn out to be unreliable and often fail to generalize outside their specific training setup.\n     2. The steerability of different concepts varies significantly, with some proving resistant to steering.\n  2. [**Typically used performance metrics overestimate steering effectiveness**](/posts/QQP4nq7TXg89CJGBh/a-sober-look-at-current-steering-methods-for-llms#Typically_used_performance_metrics_overestimate_steering_effectiveness)\n     * Most steering methods are trained and evaluated in artificial settings like Multiple Choice Question Answering instead of settings that reflect deployment, like free-form text generation or behavior on typical application tasks. This raises questions about whether steering methods are useful and reliable in practice.\n  3. [**Methods are not compared on the same benchmarks and metrics**](/posts/QQP4nq7TXg89CJGBh/a-sober-look-at-current-steering-methods-for-llms#Methods_are_not_compared_on_the_same_benchmarks_and_metrics)\n     * Most steering methods are trained and evaluated on method-specific datasets, in which they show good performance. However, evaluations on datasets of prior work or common benchmarks are often missing. This makes it difficult to track progress and evaluate the strengths and weaknesses of new methods.\n\n\n\nOverall, there is still much research to do to make steering methods a reliable and useful tool for controlling LLMs. We conclude with a discussion of several important [recommendations](/posts/QQP4nq7TXg89CJGBh/a-sober-look-at-current-steering-methods-for-llms#Recommendations) for future research.\n\n## Current steering methods have substantial limitations\n\n### Reliability of positive steering effect\n\nIn [Analyzing the Generalization and Reliability of Steering Vectors (Tan et al., 2024)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2407.12404) (accepted at NeurIPS 2024), we find that [Contrastive Activation Addition (CAA)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.06681) has substantial limitations in terms of robustness and reliability. Steerability is highly variable across different inputs: depending on the concept, spurious biases can substantially contribute to how effective steering is for each input, presenting a challenge for the widespread use of steering vectors. While CAA is effective on some tasks, many behaviors turn out to be unsteerable, even when sweeping across all layers and strengths for steering. As a result, it is difficult to ensure they will be effective on a given task of interest, limiting their reliability as a general alignment intervention.[1]\n\nIn [Comparing Bottom-Up and Top-Down Steering Approaches on In-Context Learning Tasks (Brumley et al., 2024)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.07213) (accepted at MINT @ NeurIPS 2024) we report reliability issues with both [In-Context Vectors (ICVs)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.06668) and [Function Vectors (FVs)](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.15213). ICVs perform poorly on functional tasks (i.e., tasks which generally involve invoking some sort of 'function' to transform input to output, e.g., translation) and have high variance for other tasks like sentiment transfer. On the contrary, Function Vectors (FVs) did well on functional tasks but on the sentiment transfer task, they even steer away from the desired behavior, echoing results from [Analy", "stderr": "", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false, "bashEditDiff": {"files": [], "moreFiles": 0, "shared": true}}
```

  "overall_assessment": "This is a substantially improved revision. I checked all 21 critiques from the previous round and every one of them has been addressed, most of them with a fix that is better than the one I proposed: SRS is gone entirely and replaced by three absolute main effects with an explicit no-ratio reporting rule; the full-residual patch is demoted to a positive control and replaced by the harm-component subspace rung with an orthogonalized matched-norm control; the non-safety arm is promoted from a panel member to a pre-registered gate with a second fine-tune control; the coherence interaction is now a crossed control rather than a placebo; the directions are cross-fitted; Stage 4's decision rule is 5-of-6 with a stated MDE; the target column is co-primary with harmful-compliance; the probe set is anchored in XSTest/OR-Bench with mechanical prefix generation; and the parent-fixed/refitted basis conventions are separated by where each is correct.\n\nI verified the two load-bearing prior-art claims directly. arXiv:2607.14147 exists and its pre-registered 2x2 is harm x SURFACE-trigger-density (AdvBench-scary/clean x XSTest-safe/Alpaca), not harm x prefill; it tests harmful prompts under an affirmative prefill and benign prompts under the same affirmative prefill, but never puts a matched HAZARDOUS-CONTENT continuation under a benign request, and the +16.8pp specificity figure is a behavioural difference-in-differences attached to an attention knockout ('the same early knockout on benign prompts under the prefill barely moves refusal (0%->4%), a specificity difference-in-differences of +16.8pp'). Its base-model control, its benign-residual demolition of full-residual patching, its 'any single direction reads <=0.73 and none steers' versus '~0.85' full probe, and the 0.24-vs-0.03 concentration figure are all quoted accurately in the submission. arXiv:2505.19056's 'at most 10%' vs '70-80%' and its feature-space PCA section are confirmed. So the arming interaction is a genuinely unoccupied estimand and the submission's concessions to prior work are honest rather than strategic.\n\nWhat still blocks a higher score is not novelty and not honesty; it is that several of the pre-registered tests are not yet well-posed. Three of them are load-bearing. (i) The primary harm-axis readout is never told where it is fitted, and if it is a diff-of-means over harmful-vs-harmless requests at prompt positions it is the SAME object as the abliteration direction, in which case the parent-fixed post-edit measurement of O, CB and A is annihilated by construction and Stage 2's headline is vacuous. (ii) Headline part (iii) -- CB_post larger in SafeRL than in Qwen3-4B -- is arithmetically entailed by supporting prediction P2 plus part (ii), so the test that is said to 'carry the claim' can pass entirely on a pre-edit level difference and say nothing about survival. (iii) The blanket assertion that cross-checkpoint comparisons 'need no normalisation' because they are 'the same term in the same units' is wrong: residual-stream and LayerNorm scales differ between checkpoints and change under the edit, so every cross-checkpoint ordering claim in the design, including the safety-specificity gate against Base, is scale-confounded. This last one is an over-correction from my previous round's push away from ratios; the right landing spot is a standardized effect in units of each checkpoint's OWN measured null band, which the design already computes.\n\nBeyond those, the pre-registration still contains no numbers (every delta and TOST margin is a symbol), the item count of 24 is the binding constraint on an interaction estimate in a design whose entire Stage 1 is 256 forward passes, and the coherence subtraction assumes an additivity and a matched mismatch magnitude that are neither stated nor checked. All six are fixable inside one iteration and none requires new compute. Fix them and this is a 6-7; ship it as written and Stage 2 risks producing a number that was determined by the edit's algebra rather than by the model.",
  "strengths": [
    "Every one of the previous round's 21 critiques is addressed, and several fixes are stronger than what was asked for. The matched-request-axis-damage control in particular -- comparing lineages at edit strengths chosen so the drop in O is equal, with the strength-to-damage curve measured first -- is exactly the control that arXiv:2505.19056's own prompt-centroid numbers (28.8/33.9/28.7 vs 10.0/7.7/13.7) demand, and pre-registering the comparison at matched DAMAGE rather than matched STRENGTH is a level of care above what the adjacent literature does.",
    "The novelty claim is now narrow, honest and verified. I confirmed independently that arXiv:2607.14147's pre-registered 2x2 is harm x surface-trigger-density and that it never crosses the request factor with a hazardous-content prefill; the off-diagonal cell (benign request, hazardous self-written text) is genuinely unmeasured at the activation level. The submission concedes the response-site pathway entirely rather than re-claiming it, which is the right move and the one that was missing last round.",
    "The estimand is now unambiguous. O, CB and A are written as explicit contrasts over four named cells, the aggregation order (positions -> layer band -> per item -> mean over items with an item-clustered bootstrap) is fixed before collection, and the reporting rule explicitly checks that no statistic in the design is computable only when the hypothesis is true. That last check is rare and it is the direct fix to last round's SRS-undefined-under-its-own-null problem.",
    "The design matches the mechanistic-interpretability field's own current rules almost line by line: decodability is not assumed to imply actionability and the causal gate is stated on the intervention rather than the readout; collateral disruption on already-correct benign cases and the per-item effect distribution are reported rather than a mean at one coefficient; difference-in-means and raw non-featurized hidden vectors are the baselines; nulls are measured from >=20 random-direction and shuffled-label draws run through the whole pipeline including the direction fit; and stability across folds, bootstrap draws and probe-set subsamples is a reported criterion rather than an assumption.",
    "Both outcomes are informative and the cheapest kill is checked first. 'If A is inside the null band in EVERY checkpoint, the arming coordinate is empty' is checkable at Stage 1 before any judge spend, and the run then reports a single response-site term and says plainly that arXiv:2607.14147 already owns it. The double-dissociation criterion (training raises CB not A; the prompt-fitted edit lowers A not CB) is the strongest form the result can take and cannot be produced by a single scalar of safety strength.",
    "Fidelity to the commissioned request is high. Stage 1 is explicitly named as the commissioned deliverable and covers base, official safety fine-tune and community abliterated plus two controls in one architecture with no alignment step; the metric reads activations of a single model with no parent and no benchmark; logit and text readouts appear only as labelled baselines; and the framing is where-safety-lives rather than attack selection, exactly as the invariant requires."
  ],
  "dimension_scores": [
    {
      "dimension": "fidelity",
      "score": 4,
      "justification": "The commissioned deliverable -- an activation-level comparison of Qwen3-4B base, the official safety fine-tune and the community abliterated checkpoint -- is Stage 1, is named as such, and is a superset of the three requested models (it adds the instruct model and a non-safety fine-tune as controls). The metric (O, CB, A) is read from one checkpoint's residual stream in 256 forward passes with no generation, no judge, no parent and no benchmark, which is the single-model activation-or-weights reading the invariant demands. Logit-gap, refusal-rate and card-regex readouts appear only in the baseline column. The in-house abliteration in Stage 2 is a weight edit used as a manipulation to localise where safety lives, not an attack-selection study, and the hazardous prefixes are teacher-forced measurement stimuli rather than jailbreak attempts. The one residual gap is the '0- to few prompts' phrasing: the deployed profile needs 24 quadruples plus 64 direction-fit prompts plus a coherence control, which is honestly stated but is ~150 prompts rather than 'a few', and the design never reports how far down that budget can be pushed.",
      "improvements": [
        "Add a prompt-budget learning curve to Stage 4: recompute the leave-one-family-out transfer with the profile estimated from 4, 8, 16 and all 24 quadruples, and report the smallest budget at which the profile still beats the strongest baseline. This is free (the activations are already collected) and it is the number the commissioned request literally asks for, so it converts a caveat into a headline answer.",
        "State once, in the hypothesis body, that the Stage-2 edit is a manipulation used to localise the signal and not a proposed attack, and that the harvested public abliterated checkpoint is the arm the request named. One sentence; it removes the only reading under which this looks like attack work."
      ]

    {
      "dimension": "soundness",
      "score": 3,
      "justification": "The correlational design is now tight: crossed factors over identical spans, cross-fitted directions, measured nulls, a crossed non-safety coherence control, a safety-specificity gate against two non-safety arms, a causal gate that separates a readable representation from a write-handle, and a reporting rule that forbids ratios with vanishing denominators. Against that, three pre-registered tests are not yet well-posed, and each of them is load-bearing rather than peripheral. The primary readout direction's fitting corpus and POSITIONS are never stated, which leaves open the possibility that it is collinear with the abliteration direction and that the entire post-edit measurement is annihilated by the edit's own algebra. Headline part (iii) is entailed by supporting prediction P2 and so cannot discriminate survival from level. And the repeated claim that cross-checkpoint comparisons in 'absolute internal units' need no normalisation is simply false for residual-stream projections across checkpoints with different activation scales. Add to that a pre-registration with no numbers in it, an interaction estimated at n=24 in a design where compute is effectively free, and a coherence subtraction whose additivity is unchecked, and the result is a design that is careful in its reasoning but not yet safe to execute.",
      "improvements": [
        "State where and on what the primary harm axis is fitted (corpus, label contrast, token positions), and pre-register cos(r_readout, r_ablit) as a Stage-2 gate with a threshold above which the parent-fixed post-edit arm is declared vacuous and reported only under the refit and full-probe readouts.",
        "Restate headline part (iii) as a difference-in-differences in the same units: [CB_post - CB_pre](SafeRL) - [CB_post - CB_pre](Qwen3-4B) > delta_3, paired and item-clustered at matched O-damage. No denominator, and unlike the current form it is not entailed by P2.",

        "Raise the scenario count to >=96 and justify it with a power calculation for the coherence-net interaction rather than by what the twin sets happen to yield; the interaction contrast carries ~4x the variance of a simple difference and is then reported net of a second interaction."

      "dimension": "presentation",

      "justification": "The writing is precise and the reasoning is unusually easy to follow for a document of this density: terms are defined, the three regimes are laid out explicitly, and the distinction between what is conceded to prior work and what is claimed is drawn sentence by sentence. The matched-request-axis-damage argument is restated in five separate places, the no-ratio reporting rule in four, and the 2607.14147 concession in three; meanwhile the quantities a downstream planner actually needs -- the threshold values, the layer band, the position window, the item count, the direction-fit corpus -- are either absent or buried in prose. An implementer reading this would have to reconstruct the experiment table from scratch.",

    {
      "dimension": "contribution",
      "score": 3,
      "justification": "The arming interaction is a real and, as far as I can verify, unclaimed estimand, and the methodological point behind it -- that a response-site effect measured only under harmful requests is an effect at one level of an unvaried modifier and therefore not a per-model property -- is a genuine finding-shaped claim rather than a measurement applied to more cases. The double dissociation, if it holds, would establish two separately real terms in a way no single safety scalar can produce, and the forecast direction (pre-edit arming fraction predicts post-edit survival) is a real prediction rather than a fit. The ceiling on the contribution is that the response-site pathway itself is conceded to arXiv:2607.14147 and the practical advice is conceded to arXiv:2505.19056, so what remains is one interaction term, its per-checkpoint reduction, and the safe-completion and edited arms. That is a solid workshop-to-main-track delta, not a field-moving one, and whether it lands depends entirely on whether A is measurable above the null band at the planned item count -- which the design does not yet demonstrate.",

        "Make the methodological claim the headline rather than the mechanism claim.   "critiques": [
    {
      "category": "methodology",
      "severity": "major",
      "description": "THE PRIMARY READOUT DIRECTION MAY BE THE ABLITERATION DIRECTION, WHICH WOULD MAKE THE ENTIRE STAGE-2 HEADLINE VACUOUS BY CONSTRUCTION. The hypothesis specifies the primary readout as 'projection onto the model's own harm axis, fitted by difference in means and CROSS-FITTED' over '64 short prompts to fit the readout direction' -- but it never says what the label contrast is or at which TOKEN POSITIONS the means are taken. If that direction is a difference of means over harmful versus harmless REQUESTS at prompt positions, it is the same object, produced by the same recipe, as the abliteration direction the hypothesis itself defines two paragraphs later. Abliteration orthogonalises that direction out of every matrix that writes to the residual stream, so the residual-stream component along it is driven toward zero at ALL positions, prompt and response alike. Under the PARENT-FIXED convention -- which the hypothesis pre-registers as primary for exactly this arm -- O, CB and A would then all collapse into the null band after the edit, not because the model lost a monitor but because the readout coordinate was deleted from the write path. Criterion (ii) ('CB remains above the null band after the edit in every lineage where it was above it before') would fail mechanically, criterion (i) ('A falls into the null band') would pass mechanically, and criterion (iii) would be a comparison of two near-zero residues. The hypothesis anticipates the BASIS question (it publishes cos(r_parent, r_child) and argues that refitting would change coordinates) but not the ANNIHILATION question, which is the one that decides whether Stage 2 measures anything. This is the single highest-risk item in the design: it would consume the whole Stage-2 budget and return a pre-determined answer.",
      "suggested_action": "Three things, all cheap and all before Stage 2 runs. (1) State the readout direction's definition completely: corpus, label contrast, and token positions. The version that makes the design work is a harm axis fitted at RESPONSE positions on hazardous-versus-benign continuations under a held-fixed request -- a genuinely different vector from the prompt-position request-contrast direction that abliteration removes -- and the hypothesis should say so and explain why. (2) Pre-register cos(r_readout_parent, r_ablit) as a Stage-2 gate with a stated threshold (e.g. |cos| > 0.5) above which the parent-fixed post-edit arm is declared confounded by construction and the arm is reported only under the per-checkpoint refit and the full-probe readouts, with the parent-fixed numbers shown as a labelled artefact. (3) Add a one-line component-preservation diagnostic to Stage 2: measure the variance of the edited model's residual stream along r_readout against the variance along 20 matched-norm random directions, at the same layers and positions. If the ratio is at floor, every post-edit term is an annihilation residue and must be reported as such. Also state explicitly whether the direction is refitted inside each cross-fitting fold in the edited arm or carried over from the parent's folds."
    },
    {
      "category": "rigor",
      "severity": "major",
      "description": "HEADLINE PART (iii) IS ARITHMETICALLY ENTAILED BY SUPPORTING PREDICTION P2 AND THEREFORE TESTS NOTHING ABOUT SURVIVAL. The hypothesis says part (iii) -- 'AT MATCHED REQUEST-AXIS DAMAGE ... the surviving CB_post is larger in the SafeRL lineage than in the Qwen3-4B lineage' -- 'is the comparison that carries the claim'. But P2 already predicts CB(SafeRL) >> CB(Qwen3-4B) BEFORE any edit, and it is pre-registered as an ordering criterion in its own right ('CB(SafeRL) - CB(Qwen3-4B) > delta_2'). Part (ii) then asserts that the edit leaves CB above the null band in both lineages. If both hold, CB_post(SafeRL) > CB_post(Qwen3-4B) follows from the pre-edit gap alone; the edit could damage CB equally in both lineages, or damage it MORE in SafeRL, and (iii) would still pass. So the test that is designated as load-bearing is confounded with the level difference it is supposed to be independent of, and the sentence 'the pre-edit arming fraction of each lineage is recorded BEFORE the edit so that post-edit survival is a forecast rather than a fit' is not delivered by any registered criterion. The claim the hypothesis actually wants -- 'what an abliteration leaves behind is the unarmed part, so a checkpoint whose safety was unarmed to begin with loses less of it' -- is a claim about DIFFERENTIAL LOSS, not about post-edit level.",
      "suggested_action": "Restate part (iii) as a difference-in-differences in absolute internal units, which has no denominator and therefore does not violate the no-ratio rule: [CB_post - CB_pre](SafeRL) - [CB_post - CB_pre](Qwen3-4B) > delta_3, estimated paired over items with an item-clustered bootstrap, at the pre-registered matched-O-damage level, with delta_3 given a number and an attainability check. Keep the current level comparison as a labelled descriptive secondary. Additionally pre-register the forecast form explicitly: rank the two lineages by pre-edit A (or by the pre-edit arming fraction where it is defined) and state that the lineage with the SMALLER pre-edit A must show the SMALLER absolute drop in CB -- that is the statement that makes the pre-edit measurement a forecast, and it is currently only asserted in prose."
    },
    {
      "category": "rigor",
      "severity": "major",
      "description": "'ABSOLUTE INTERNAL UNITS' ARE NOT COMMENSURABLE ACROSS CHECKPOINTS, AND THE DESIGN NOW ASSERTS FOUR TIMES THAT THEY ARE. The submission repeatedly argues that cross-checkpoint comparisons need no normalisation because they are 'the same term, in the same units, under the same recipe' -- 'so the comparison needs no normalisation', 'which is also the answer to the objection that drops in two different main effects are not commensurable'. That reasoning is valid for comparing two different terms WITHIN one checkpoint. It is not valid ACROSS checkpoints. A projection onto a unit direction is measured in residual-stream units, and residual-stream norms, LayerNorm gains and per-layer activation scales differ systematically between a base model, an instruction-tuned model, an RL-tuned model and a weight-edited model -- and abliteration changes them again by removing a rank-one component from every writing matrix. Every cross-checkpoint claim in the design inherits this: the safety-specificity GATE (Qwen3-4B's A must exceed Base's A 'by a margin fixed in advance, in absolute internal units'), the ordering criteria A(Qwen3-4B) - A(SafeRL) and CB(SafeRL) - CB(Qwen3-4B), and the CB_post comparison. A checkpoint with a 20% larger residual norm will show 20% larger O, CB and A with no difference in mechanism. This is an over-correction from my previous round: I pushed the design off ratios between two different main effects, and it has landed on raw un-normalised levels, which for cross-model comparison is the worse of the two errors.",
      "suggested_action": "Adopt a third option the design already computes and does not use. The >=20 random-direction and shuffled-label draws give each checkpoint its own null-band standard deviation in its own units; dividing every term by that checkpoint's null SD yields a standardized effect that is neither a ratio between two different main effects nor a raw level. Pre-register the standardized form as PRIMARY for every cross-checkpoint comparison (the specificity gate, both orderings, the CB_post DiD) and the raw form as primary for within-checkpoint ones, and report both side by side everywhere. Add a mandatory descriptive table of per-checkpoint mean residual-stream norm and LayerNorm scale at the frozen band and positions, so a reader can see how large the scale differences actually are; if they are negligible the raw comparisons are vindicated cheaply, and if they are not, the design has already protected itself."
    },
    {
      "category": "rigor",

      "description": "THE PRE-REGISTRATION CONTAINS NO NUMBERS. The hypothesis is the pre-registration document, and it commits repeatedly to thresholds that are never given values: 'delta_1 and delta_2 fixed before the run', 'a margin fixed in advance' (the safety-specificity gate), 'by a margin fixed in advance' (headline iii), 'TOST against a margin fixed in advance' (headline i), 'with a pass threshold fixed in advance' (split-half cosine for direction stability), 'the minimum detectable effect ... computed and reported ... BEFORE the sweep'. Every one of these is a symbol. A threshold that is named but not valued is not pre-registered -- it can be set after the layer-by-position maps are seen, which is exactly the freedom the rest of the document works hard to remove. The same applies to the two quantitative gates that DO have numbers (the distinct-3-gram ratio of 0.6 and the similarity gap of 0.15 in the template-collapse diagnostic, and the +/-25% TOST margin for the placebo): those are the model for what the rest should look like, and they show the author knows how to do it.",
      "suggested_action": "Add a criteria table to success_criteria with one row per pass/fail test and a NUMBER in the threshold column, expressed in the standardized null-SD units of the previous critique so the values are interpretable before any data exist: e.g. safety-specificity margin = 1.0 null-SD; delta_1 = delta_2 = 1.0 null-SD; delta_3 = 0.75 null-SD; TOST equivalence margin for post-edit A = +/-0.5 null-SD; split-half cosine pass threshold = 0.7. For each row, show the arithmetic that the margin is attainable at the planned item count given the bootstrap SE you expect, exactly as the document already promises to do for the placebo TOST. Also fix the frozen layer band and position window as a rule stated now (e.g. 'the contiguous 25%-depth band maximising the Stage-0 positive control', 'response tokens 5-40'), not as a decision to be made at Stage 0 with unstated criteria."
    },
    {
      "category": "rigor",
      "severity": "major",
      "description": "n = 24 IS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT ON AN INTERACTION ESTIMATE IN A DESIGN WHOSE COMPUTE IS EFFECTIVELY FREE. The headline quantity A is an INTERACTION contrast, whose variance under equal cell variances is four times that of a simple difference; it is then reported NET of a second interaction (the coherence control), which adds variance again; and it is estimated under 6-fold cross-fitting, which further inflates the standard error because each fold's direction is fitted on 20 items. On top of that, criterion (i) requires an EQUIVALENCE test (post-edit A inside the null band by TOST), which is the test most punished by small n, and the stability criterion asks for consistent sign across folds, bootstrap draws and at least three probe-set subsamples of a 24-item set. Nothing in the document justifies 24; it is inherited from the previous iteration. Meanwhile the whole of Stage 1 is stated as 256 forward passes with zero generated tokens on a 20 GB card -- seconds of GPU time. There is no resource argument for the number that determines every interval, every margin and every MDE in the design. The document's own escape hatch ('if 24 scenarios do not reach it, the scenario count is raised before Stage 1 runs') is conditioned only on the split-half direction-stability check, not on power for A.",
      "suggested_action": "Set the item count from a power calculation for the COHERENCE-NET ARMING TERM at the smallest effect you would be willing to call real (state it in null-SD units), not from what the twin sets happen to yield, and report the resulting n and MDE in the hypothesis. Concretely I would plan for >=96 scenarios: at 4 cells x 2 crossings that is ~1,500 forward passes per checkpoint, still minutes, and it roughly halves every interval in the design. If the matched-twin supply is the limiting factor rather than compute, say so explicitly and state the largest attainable n and the MDE it delivers -- an honest 'this design can detect A >= X and no smaller' is worth more than an unqualified n=24. Make the Stage-0 escape hatch conditional on the power check as well as on the split-half cosine."
    },
    {
      "category": "methodology",
      "severity": "major",
      "description": "THE COHERENCE SUBTRACTION ASSUMES ADDITIVITY AND A MATCHED MISMATCH MAGNITUDE, NEITHER OF WHICH IS CHECKED. The crossed non-safety control is the right instrument and its addition is the best single fix in this revision, but 'the safety arming term is reported net of it' is a stronger operation than the control licenses. Subtracting the coherence interaction from the safety interaction is valid only if (a) the generic instruction-mismatch signal enters the readout additively and (b) the two crossings produce mismatches of comparable MAGNITUDE. Neither is plausible a priori: a benign topic-A request followed by a benign topic-B prefix is a mild non-sequitur, whereas a benign request followed by a hazardous prefix is a severe one, so the coherence control could under-estimate the generic component by a large factor and leave a residual 'arming' term that is pure mismatch. The hypothesis itself identifies this as 'the exact term the headline is stated in', which makes the calibration of the control load-bearing rather than incidental.",
      "suggested_action": "Calibrate the two crossings on a measurable proxy for mismatch magnitude that costs nothing: the model's own mean token log-probability of the PREFIX given the REQUEST, which is already available from the same forward passes. Report the four-cell log-probability table for BOTH 2x2s as a mandatory Stage-0 diagnostic, and pre-register that the off-diagonal log-probability penalty in the coherence crossing must be statistically indistinguishable from (or larger than) the penalty in the safety crossing before the subtraction is performed. If they do not match, do not subtract a constant -- regress the per-item readout on the per-item log-probability penalty across all eight cells and report A as the coefficient net of that covariate, which is robust to a magnitude mismatch and to mild non-additivity. State in the disconfirming section that if the coherence penalty cannot be brought up to the safety penalty, the coherence-net A is reported as an upper bound rather than as an estimate."
    },
    {
      "category": "methodology",
      "severity": "major",
      "description": "'THE MODEL'S REFUSAL DRIVE' IS THE OUTCOME OF THE CAUSAL ARM AND OF HALF THE SAFETY-SPECIFICITY GATE, AND IT IS NEVER DEFINED. The causal criterion reads 'adding or removing the harm-direction component at response positions changes the internal refusal drive more than a matched-norm orthogonalized control', and the CB half of the gate reads 'moving the harm component at response positions must change the refusal drive in the safety-trained checkpoint and not in Base'. Nothing in the document says what that quantity is. The three candidates behave very differently: projection onto a refusal direction is the readout the document itself DEMOTES to a baseline on the strength of 2607.14147's 'any single direction reads <=0.73 and none steers'; the full cross-validated probe's decision score is defensible but is trained on a decision label that Base does not have; and the log-probability of refusal-opening tokens under teacher forcing is the most interpretable but is a logit-level quantity, which the commissioned invariant allows as the OUTCOME of an activation-level intervention but which the document should say out loud. Since a gate cannot be evaluated against an undefined outcome, this blocks the criterion that decides whether CB is called a monitor at all.",
      "suggested_action": "Define it as a primary plus one secondary, in the hypothesis. I would make the primary the teacher-forced probability mass on a fixed refusal-onset token set at the next position after the intervened span -- it is well defined in Base, it is the quantity that 2607.14147's 0.24-vs-0.03 concentration figure speaks to, and it is an output-side READOUT of an activation-side INTERVENTION, which is exactly what the invariant permits. Make the full-probe decision score the secondary and state that the probe is trained on the instruct model and applied unchanged to Base, with that transfer flagged. Add one sentence to the methods saying that the metric itself remains activation-only and that the refusal-drive outcome appears solely as the dependent variable of the causal arm, so no reader mistakes it for part of the deployed profile."
    },
    {
      "category": "methodology",
      "severity": "minor",
      "description": "ONE PREFIX TEMPLATE AND ONE RESPONSE DEPTH MEAN THE ARMING TERM IS ESTIMATED FROM A SINGLE INPUT DISTRIBUTION. All 96 safety cells are generated 'MECHANICALLY from one fixed template applied to each pair'. Mechanical generation is the right call for auditability and it answers the previous round's probe-set critique, but it buys reproducibility at the cost of generality: the standing rule in this field is that a result reported from one input distribution is a single draw from an equivalence class rather than a property, and the stability criterion as written (folds, bootstrap resamples, probe-set subsamples) re-samples the SAME template. If the template announces the hazard explicitly ('Here is how to...'), A may be a property of that announcement rather than of the content. The same applies to response depth: a single frozen position window cannot distinguish an arming term from a transient at one depth, and depth is precisely the axis the shallow-alignment literature has claimed.",
      "suggested_action": "Author a second, structurally different prefix family -- one in which the hazard is enacted rather than announced -- and generate the cells under both templates, treating template as a factor and pre-registering that the arming ORDERING must hold under both. Separately, run the same cells at two response depths (e.g. the readout window starting ~5 tokens in and ~40 tokens in) and report A as a function of depth. Cost is 96 -> ~380 forward passes per checkpoint, still trivial, and the depth curve is a second publishable result that separates arming from shallow-alignment decay at no extra design cost."

The same applies to the two quantitative gates that DO have numbers (the distinct-3-gram ratio of 0.6 and the similarity gap of 0.15 in the template-collapse diagnostic, and the +/-25% TOST margin for the placebo): those are the model for what the rest should look like, and they show the author knows how to do it.\",\n      \"suggested_action\": \"Add a criteria table to success_criteria with one row per pass/fail test and a NUMBER in the threshold column, expressed in the standardized null-SD units of the previous critique so the values are interpretable before any data exist: e.g. safety-specificity margin = 1.0 null-SD; delta_1 = delta_2 = 1.0 null-SD; delta_3 = 0.75 null-SD; TOST equivalence margin for post-edit A = +/-0.5 null-SD; split-half cosine pass threshold = 0.7. For each row, show the arithmetic that the margin is attainable at the planned item count given the bootstrap SE you expect, exactly as the document already promises to do for the placebo TOST. Also fix the frozen layer band and position window as a rule stated now (e.g. 'the contiguous 25%-depth band maximising the Stage-0 positive control', 'response tokens 5-40'), not as a decision to be made at Stage 0 with unstated criteria.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"category\": \"rigor\",\n      \"severity\": \"major\",\n      \"description\": \"n = 24 IS THE BINDING CONSTRAINT ON AN INTERACTION ESTIMATE IN A DESIGN WHOSE COMPUTE IS EFFECTIVELY FREE. The headline quantity A is an INTERACTION contrast, whose variance under equal cell variances is four times that of a simple difference; it is then reported NET of a second interaction (the coherence control), which adds variance again; and it is estimated under 6-fold cross-fitting, which further inflates the standard error because each fold's direction is fitted on 20 items. On top of that, criterion (i) requires an EQUIVALENCE test (post-edit A inside the null band by TOST), which is the test most punished by small n, and the stability criterion asks for consistent sign across folds, bootstrap draws and at least three probe-set subsamples of a 24-item set. Nothing in the document justifies 24; it is inherited from the previous iteration. Meanwhile the whole of Stage 1 is stated as 256 forward passes with zero generated tokens on a 20 GB card -- seconds of GPU time. There is no resource argument for the number that determines every interval, every margin and every MDE in the design. The document's own escape hatch ('if 24 scenarios do not reach it, the scenario count is raised before Stage 1 runs') is conditioned only on the split-half direction-stability check, not on power for A.\",\n      \"suggested_action\": \"Set the item count from a power calculation for the COHERENCE-NET ARMING TERM at the smallest effect you would be willing to call real (state it in null-SD units), not from what the twin sets happen to yield, and report the resulting n and MDE in the hypothesis. Concretely I would plan for >=96 scenarios: at 4 cells x 2 crossings that is ~1,500 forward passes per checkpoint, still minutes, and it roughly halves every interval in the design. If the matched-twin supply is the limiting factor rather than compute, say so explicitly and state the largest attainable n and the MDE it delivers -- an honest 'this design can detect A >= X and no smaller' is worth more than an unqualified n=24. Make the Stage-0 escape hatch conditional on the power check as well as on the split-half cosine.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"category\": \"methodology\",\n      \"severity\": \"major\",\n      \"description\": \"THE COHERENCE SUBTRACTION ASSUMES ADDITIVITY AND A MATCHED MISMATCH MAGNITUDE, NEITHER OF WHICH IS CHECKED. The crossed non-safety control is the right instrument and its addition is the best single fix in this revision, but 'the safety arming term is reported net of it' is a stronger operation than the control licenses. Subtracting the coherence interaction from the safety interaction is valid only if (a) the generic instruction-mismatch signal enters the readout additively and (b) the two crossings produce mismatches of comparable MAGNITUDE. Neither is plausible a priori: a benign topic-A request followed by a benign topic-B prefix is a mild non-sequitur, whereas a benign request followed by a hazardous prefix is a severe one, so the coherence control could under-estimate the generic component by a large factor and leave a residual 'arming' term that is pure mismatch. The hypothesis itself identifies this as 'the exact term the headline is stated in', which makes the calibration of the control load-bearing rather than incidental.\",\n      \"suggested_action\": \"Calibrate the two crossings on a measurable proxy for mismatch magnitude that costs nothing: the model's own mean token log-probability of the PREFIX given the REQUEST, which is already available from the same forward passes. Report the four-cell log-probability table for BOTH 2x2s as a mandatory Stage-0 diagnostic, and pre-register that the off-diagonal log-probability penalty in the coherence crossing must be statistically indistinguishable from (or larger than) the penalty in the safety crossing before the subtraction is performed. If they do not match, do not subtract a constant -- regress the per-item readout on the per-item log-probability penalty across all eight cells and report A as the coefficient net of that covariate, which is robust to a magnitude mismatch and to mild non-additivity. State in the disconfirming section that if the coherence penalty cannot be brought up to the safety penalty, the coherence-net A is reported as an upper bound rather than as an estimate.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"category\": \"methodology\",\n      \"severity\": \"major\",\n      \"description\": \"'THE MODEL'S REFUSAL DRIVE' IS THE OUTCOME OF THE CAUSAL ARM AND OF HALF THE SAFETY-SPECIFICITY GATE, AND IT IS NEVER DEFINED. The causal criterion reads 'adding or removing the harm-direction component at response positions changes the internal refusal drive more than a matched-norm orthogonalized control', and the CB half of the gate reads 'moving the harm component at response positions must change the refusal drive in the safety-trained checkpoint and not in Base'. Nothing in the document says what that quantity is. The three candidates behave very differently: projection onto a refusal direction is the readout the document itself DEMOTES to a baseline on the strength of 2607.14147's 'any single direction reads <=0.73 and none steers'; the full cross-validated probe's decision score is defensible but is trained on a decision label that Base does not have; and the log-probability of refusal-opening tokens under teacher forcing is the most interpretable but is a logit-level quantity, which the commissioned invariant allows as the OUTCOME of an activation-level intervention but which the document should say out loud. Since a gate cannot be evaluated against an undefined outcome, this blocks the criterion that decides whether CB is called a monitor at all.\",\n      \"suggested_action\": \"Define it as a primary plus one secondary, in the hypothesis. I would make the primary the teacher-forced probability mass on a fixed refusal-onset token set at the next position after the intervened span -- it is well defined in Base, it is the quantity that 2607.14147's 0.24-vs-0.03 concentration figure speaks to, and it is an output-side READOUT of an activation-side INTERVENTION, which is exactly what the invariant permits. Make the full-probe decision score the secondary and state that the probe is trained on the instruct model and applied unchanged to Base, with that transfer flagged. Add one sentence to the methods saying that the metric itself remains activation-only and that the refusal-drive outcome appears solely as the dependent variable of the causal arm, so no reader mistakes it for part of the deployed profile.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"category\": \"methodology\",\n      \"severity\": \"minor\",\n      \"description\": \"ONE PREFIX TEMPLATE AND ONE RESPONSE DEPTH MEAN THE ARMING TERM IS ESTIMATED FROM A SINGLE INPUT DISTRIBUTION. All 96 safety cells are generated 'MECHANICALLY from one fixed template applied to each pair'. Mechanical generation is the right call for auditability and it answers the previous round's probe-set critique, but it buys reproducibility at the cost of generality: the standing rule in this field is that a result reported from one input distribution is a single draw from an equivalence class rather than a property, and the stability criterion as written (folds, bootstrap resamples, probe-set subsamples) re-samples the SAME template. If the template announces the hazard explicitly ('Here is how to...'), A may be a property of that announcement rather than of the content. The same applies to response depth: a single frozen position window cannot distinguish an arming term from a transient at one depth, and depth is precisely the axis the shallow-alignment literature has claimed.\",\n      \"suggested_action\": \"Author a second, structurally different prefix family -- one in which the hazard is enacted rather than announced -- and generate the cells under both templates, treating template as a factor and pre-registering that the arming ORDERING must hold under both. Separately, run the same cells at two response depths (e.g. the readout window starting ~5 tokens in and ~40 tokens in) and report A as a function of depth. Cost is 96 -> ~380 forward passes per checkpoint, still trivial, and the depth curve is a second publishable result that separates arming from shallow-alignment decay at no extra design cost.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"category\": \"methodology\",\n      \"severity\": \"minor\",\n      \"description\": \"THE FROZEN LAYER BAND IS CHOSEN FROM THE INSTRUCT MODEL AND THEN APPLIED TO FOUR HETEROGENEOUS CHECKPOINTS, WHICH CAN MANUFACTURE A PASS ON THE SAFETY-SPECIFICITY GATE. Stage 0 says 'The layer band and the response position window are chosen here, from sanity-gate data only, and frozen', and the sanity gate is 'a positive control that the harm readout separates the request factor at the last prompt token in the INSTRUCT model'. Post-training method is known to reshape where refusal is computed, and Base and the two edited checkpoints are exactly the arms where the instruct model's band is least likely to be right. A null CB or A in Base read in the instruct model's band is then ambiguous between 'Base has no such term' and 'Base has it somewhere else', and the first reading is what passes the safety-specificity gate. Since the gate is what licenses calling any term 'safety', a band artefact there propagates to every downstream claim.\",\n      \"suggested_action\": \"Freeze the band as a DEPTH FRACTION rather than absolute layer indices (all five checkpoints share the architecture, so this costs nothing but it is the correct convention if a non-Qwen fallback control is ever used), and add a pre-registered per-checkpoint BEST-BAND secondary: for each non-safety arm, report the maximum of the term over all bands with the Holm correction applied, and require that the safety-specificity gate hold against that maximum, not only against the frozen band. The layer-by-position maps are already being produced for all five checkpoints, so this is purely a reporting commitment. Say explicitly in the disconfirming section that a null in a non-safety arm that disappears under the best-band search is reported as a band artefact rather than as specificity.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"category\": \"evidence\",\n      \"severity\": \"minor\",\n      \"description\": \"THE FIFTH ARM'S FALLBACK WOULD SILENTLY CHANGE WHAT THE GATE TESTS. The selection rule for the non-safety fine-tune is now stated, which fixes the previous round's concern about it being dropped. But the fallback -- 'an ungated non-safety fine-tune of the nearest comparable parent in another family, reported with the caveat that parent is no longer held fixed' -- does more damage than the caveat admits. The arm's whole job is to separate 'has a response-conditioned read' from 'has safety training' by holding the parent fixed and varying only whether the fine-tune was safety-directed. Across families, tokenizer, depth, residual scale and chat template all move at once, and under the raw-units convention (see the normalisation critique) the comparison becomes uninterpretable rather than merely weakened. A code or math fine-tune of Qwen3-4B-Base also differs from the instruct checkpoint in instruction-following ability and chat template, which is a second confound the current text does not name.\",\n      \"suggested_action\": \"State the fallback ladder in full and in order: (1) ungated non-safety fine-tune of Qwen3-4B-Base; (2) failing that, an in-house LoRA or short full fine-tune of Qwen3-4B-Base on an ungated non-safety instruction set (this is hours of GPU time on a 4B model and holds parent, tokenizer and template exactly fixed, which the cross-family fallback does not); (3) only failing both, the cross-family option, and then only under the standardized null-SD units. Name the chat-template and instruction-following difference as a stated confound for any third-party fine-tune, and report the arm's chat template alongside its results.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"category\": \"clarity\",\n      \"severity\": \"minor\",\n      \"description\": \"THE DOCUMENT ARGUES WHERE IT SHOULD SPECIFY, AND AN IMPLEMENTER CANNOT BUILD FROM IT AS WRITTEN. The matched-request-axis-damage rationale appears in the hypothesis body, the motivation, the investigation approach, the success criteria and the related-works entry for 2505.19056 -- five times, in nearly identical words. The no-ratio reporting rule appears four times. The concession to 2607.14147 appears three times. Roughly a third of the text is duplication, and it displaces the content a downstream planner needs: the direction-fit corpus, the threshold values, the band and window definitions, the item count rationale, and the definition of refusal drive are all either missing or one clause long. The effect is that the document reads as a rebuttal to the previous review rather than as an experiment specification, which is a real cost when the next consumer is a planner rather than a reviewer.\",\n      \"suggested_action\": \"Cut each repeated argument to a single statement and cross-reference it. Reinvest the space in the two tables named under presentation (estimand table, criteria table with numeric thresholds) plus a short 'operational parameters' block listing: direction-fit corpus and positions, item count and its power justification, layer band and position window rules, refusal-drive definition, the five checkpoint identifiers with their exact HuggingFace repo ids, and the edit-strength grid. A planner should be able to implement Stage 0 and Stage 1 from that block alone.\"\n    },\n    {\n      \"category\": \"novelty\",\n      \"severity\": \"minor\",\n      \"description\": \"TWO LOAD-BEARING CITATIONS ARE REFERRED TO BY DESCRIPTION RATHER THAN BY IDENTIFIER. The document twice invokes 'the field's sharpest recent negative result ... a large gap between internal decodability and output-level actionability' and 'the field's clearest recent negative result on decodability versus actionability' as the justification for pre-registering the gate on the causal arm rather than on the readout -- which is the correct move and the one that most protects this design -- but never names the source. The relevant result (linear probes at 98.2% AUROC against 45.1% output sensitivity, a 53-point knowledge-action gap, with SAE feature steering indistinguishable from random perturbation) is arXiv:2603.18353, and it is one clinical domain on Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, which is a scope caveat worth stating rather than hiding. Separately, the online/self-monitoring line the design positions against has at least two more members not in related_works (arXiv:2607.02510 online safety monitoring from internal signals; arXiv:2502.05242 on making models easier to monitor from their own representations) alongside the SafeSwitch/RISA/output-aware trio already cited.\",\n      \"suggested_action\": \"Add arXiv:2603.18353 to related_works with its numbers and its scope caveat, and cite it at both places where the decodability-versus-actionability argument is made; it converts the design's most defensible methodological choice from an appeal to consensus into a grounded one. Add one-sentence entries for arXiv:2607.02510 and arXiv:2502.05242 in the same style as the existing output-aware-guardrail entry, saying that they read internal signals to monitor a model from outside while this work asks whether the model's own suppression is already output-conditioned and whether that conditioning is request-gated.\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"results_reported\": false,\n  \"coverage\": \"full\",\n  \"blocking\": false,\n  \"score\": 5,\n  \"confidence\": 4,\n  \"relation_type\": \"embedding\",\n  \"relation_rationale\": \"Same open/closed-loop frame; the old (O,C) profile is now the special case CB+A at one request level, with A the new primary.\"\n}\n", "structuredPatch": [{"oldStart": 1, "oldLines": 6, "newStart": 1, "newLines": 133, "lines": [" {", "-  \"overall_assessment\": \"DRAFT - pending prior-art agent results.\",", "-  \"strengths\": [],", "-  \"critiques\": [],", "-  \"score\": 5", "+  \"overall_assessment\": \"This is a substantially improved revision. I checked all 21 critiques from the previous round and every one of them has been addressed, most of them with a fix that is better than the one I proposed: SRS is gone entirely and replaced by three absolute main effects with an explicit no-ratio reporting rule; the full-residual patch is demoted to a positive control and replaced by the harm-component subspace rung with an orthogonalized matched-norm control; the non-safety arm is promoted from a panel member to a pre-registered gate with a second fine-tune control; the coherence interaction is now a crossed control rather than a placebo; the directions are cross-fitted; Stage 4's decision rule is 5-of-6 with a stated MDE; the target column is co-primary with harmful-compliance; the probe set is anchored in XSTest/OR-Bench with mechanical prefix generation; and the parent-fixed/refitted basis conventions are separated by where each is correct.\\n\\nI verified the two load-bearing prior-art claims directly. arXiv:2607.14147 exists and its pre-registered 2x2 is harm x SURFACE-trigger-density (AdvBench-scary/clean x XSTest-safe/Alpaca), not harm x prefill; it tests harmful prompts under an affirmative prefill and benign prompts under the same affirmative prefill, but never puts a matched HAZARDOUS-CONTENT continuation under a benign request, and the +16.8pp specificity figure is a behavioural difference-in-differences attached to an attention knockout ('the same early knockout on benign prompts under the prefill barely moves refusal (0%->4%), a specificity difference-in-differences of +16.8pp'). Its base-model control, its benign-residual demolition of full-residual patching, its 'any single direction reads <=0.73 and none steers' versus '~0.85' full probe, and the 0.24-vs-0.03 concentration figure are all quoted accurately in the submission. arXiv:2505.19056's 'at most 10%' vs '70-80%' and its feature-space PCA section are confirmed. So the arming interaction is a genuinely unoccupied estimand and the submission's concessions to prior work are honest rather than strategic.\\n\\nWhat still blocks a higher score is not novelty and not honesty; it is that several of the pre-registered tests are not yet well-posed. Three of them are load-bearing. (i) The primary harm-axis readout is never told where it is fitted, and if it is a diff-of-means over harmful-vs-harmless requests at prompt positions it is the SAME object as the abliteration direction, in which case the parent-fixed post-edit measurement of O, CB and A is annihilated by construction and Stage 2's headline is vacuous. (ii) Headline part (iii) -- CB_post larger in SafeRL than in Qwen3-4B -- is arithmetically entailed by supporting prediction P2 plus part (ii), so the test that is said to 'carry the claim' can pass entirely on a pre-edit level difference and say nothing about survival. (iii) The blanket assertion that cross-checkpoint comparisons 'need no normalisation' because they are 'the same term in the same units' is wrong: residual-stream and LayerNorm scales differ between checkpoints and change under the edit, so every cross-checkpoint ordering claim in the design, including the safety-specificity gate against Base, is scale-confounded. This last one is an over-correction from my previous round's push away from ratios; the right landing spot is a standardized effect in units of each checkpoint's OWN measured null band, which the design already computes.\\n\\nBeyond those, the pre-registration still contains no numbers (every delta and TOST margin is a symbol), the item count of 24 is the binding constraint on an interaction estimate in a design whose entire Stage 1 is 256 forward passes, and the coherence subtraction assumes an additivity and a matched mismatch magnitude that are neither stated nor checked. All six are fixable inside one iteration and none requires new compute. Fix them and this is a 6-7; ship it as written and Stage 2 risks producing a number that was determined by the edit's algebra rather than by the model.\",", "+  \"strengths\": [", "+    \"Every one of the previous round's 21 critiques is addressed, and several fixes are stronger than what was asked for. The matched-request-axis-damage control in particular -- comparing lineages at edit strengths chosen so the drop in O is equal, with the strength-to-damage curve measured first -- is exactly the control that arXiv:2505.19056's own prompt-centroid numbers (28.8/33.9/28.7 vs 10.0/7.7/13.7) demand, and pre-registering the comparison at matched DAMAGE rather than matched STRENGTH is a level of care above what the adjacent literature does.\",", "+    \"The novelty claim is now narrow, honest and verified. I confirmed independently that arXiv:2607.14147's pre-registered 2x2 is harm x surface-trigger-density and that it never crosses the request factor with a hazardous-content prefill; the off-diagonal cell (benign request, hazardous self-written text) is genuinely unmeasured at the activation level. The submission concedes the response-site pathway entirely rather than re-claiming it, which is the right move and the one that was missing last round.\",", "+    \"The estimand is now unambiguous. O, CB and A are written as explicit contrasts over four named cells, the aggregation order (positions -> layer band -> per item -> mean over items with an item-clustered bootstrap) is fixed before collection, and the reporting rule explicitly checks that no statistic in the design is computable only when the hypothesis is true. That last check is rare and it is the direct fix to last round's SRS-undefined-under-its-own-null problem.\",", "+    \"The design matches the mechanistic-interpretability field's own current rules almost line by line: decodability is not assumed to imply actionability and the causal gate is stated on the intervention rather than the readout; collateral disruption on already-correct benign cases and the per-item effect distribution are reported rather than a mean at one coefficient; difference-in-means and raw non-featurized hidden vectors are the baselines; nulls are measured from >=20 random-direction and shuffled-label draws run through the whole pipeline including the direction fit; and stability across folds, bootstrap draws and probe-set subsamples is a reported criterion rather than an assumption.\",", "+    \"Both outcomes are informative and the cheapest kill is checked first. 'If A is inside the null band in EVERY checkpoint, the arming coordinate is empty' is checkable at Stage 1 before any judge spend, and the run then reports a single response-site term and says plainly that arXiv:2607.14147 already owns it. The double-dissociation criterion (training raises CB not A; the prompt-fitted edit lowers A not CB) is the strongest form the result can take and cannot be produced by a single scalar of safety strength.\",", "+    \"Fidelity to the commissioned request is high. Stage 1 is explicitly named as the commissioned deliverable and covers base, official safety fine-tune and community abliterated plus two controls in one architecture with no alignment step; the metric reads activations of a single model with no parent and no benchmark; logit and text readouts appear only as labelled baselines; and the framing is where-safety-lives rather than attack selection, exactly as the invariant requires.\"", "+  ],", "+  \"dimension_scores\": [", "+    {", "+      \"dimension\": \"fidelity\",", "+      \"score\": 4,", "+      \"justification\": \"The commissioned deliverable -- an activation-level comparison of Qwen3-4B base, the official safety fine-tune and the community abliterated checkpoint -- is Stage 1, is named as such, and is a superset of the three requested models (it adds the instruct model and a non-safety fine-tune as controls). The metric (O, CB, A) is read from one checkpoint's residual stream in 256 forward passes with no generation, no judge, no parent and no benchmark, which is the single-model activation-or-weights reading the invariant demands. Logit-gap, refusal-rate and card-regex readouts appear only in the baseline column. The in-house abliteration in Stage 2 is a weight edit used as a manipulation to localise where safety lives, not an attack-selection study, and the hazardous prefixes are teacher-forced measurement stimuli rather than jailbreak attempts. The one residual gap is the '0- to few prompts' phrasing: the deployed profile needs 24 quadruples plus 64 direction-fit prompts plus a coherence control, which is honestly stated but is ~150 prompts rather than 'a few', and the design never reports how far down that budget can be pushed.\",", "+      \"improvements\": [", "+        \"Add a prompt-budget learning curve to Stage 4: recompute the leave-one-family-out transfer with the profile estimated from 4, 8, 16 and all 24 quadruples, and report the smallest budget at which the profile still beats the strongest baseline. This is free (the activations are already collected) and it is the number the commissioned request literally asks for, so it converts a caveat into a headline answer.\",", "+        \"State once, in the hypothesis body, that the Stage-2 edit is a manipulation used to localise the signal and not a proposed attack, and that the harvested public abliterated checkpoint is the arm the request named. One sentence; it removes the only reading under which this looks like attack work.\"", "+      ]", "+    },", "+    {", "+      \"dimension\": \"soundness\",", "+      \"score\": 3,", "+      \"justification\": \"The correlational design is now tight: crossed factors over identical spans, cross-fitted directions, measured nulls, a crossed non-safety coherence control, a safety-specificity gate against two non-safety arms, a causal gate that separates a readable representation from a write-handle, and a reporting rule that forbids ratios with vanishing denominators. Against that, three pre-registered tests are not yet well-posed, and each of them is load-bearing rather than peripheral. The primary readout direction's fitting corpus and POSITIONS are never stated, which leaves open the possibility that it is collinear with the abliteration direction and that the entire post-edit measurement is annihilated by the edit's own algebra. Headline part (iii) is entailed by supporting prediction P2 and so cannot discriminate survival from level. And the repeated claim that cross-checkpoint comparisons in 'absolute internal units' need no normalisation is simply false for residual-stream projections across checkpoints with different activation scales. Add to that a pre-registration with no numbers in it, an interaction estimated at n=24 in a design where compute is effectively free, and a coherence subtraction whose additivity is unchecked, and the result is a design that is careful in its reasoning but not yet safe to execute.\",", "+      \"improvements\": [", "+        \"State where and on what the primary harm axis is fitted (corpus, label contrast, token positions), and pre-register cos(r_readout, r_ablit) as a Stage-2 gate with a threshold above which the parent-fixed post-edit arm is declared vacuous and reported only under the refit and full-probe readouts.\",", "+        \"Restate headline part (iii) as a difference-in-differences in the same units: [CB_post - CB_pre](SafeRL) - [CB_post - CB_pre](Qwen3-4B) > delta_3, paired and item-clustered at matched O-damage. No denominator, and unlike the current form it is not entailed by P2.\",", "+        \"Standardize every CROSS-checkpoint term by that checkpoint's own measured null-band SD (already computed from the >=20 random-direction draws) and pre-register the standardized version as primary for cross-checkpoint claims, keeping raw units for within-checkpoint ones.\",", "+        \"Put actual numbers on delta_1, delta_2, delta_3, the TOST margins, the safety-specificity margin and the split-half cosine threshold, expressed in null-band SD units, with the attainability arithmetic shown at the planned item count.\",", "+        \"Raise the scenario count to >=96 and justify it with a power calculation for the coherence-net interaction rather than by what the twin sets happen to yield; the interaction contrast carries ~4x the variance of a simple difference and is then reported net of a second interaction.\"", "+      ]", "+    },", "+    {", "+      \"dimension\": \"presentation\",", "+      \"score\": 3,", "+      \"justification\": \"The writing is precise and the reasoning is unusually easy to follow for a document of this density: terms are defined, the three regimes are laid out explicitly, and the distinction between what is conceded to prior work and what is claimed is drawn sentence by sentence. But it is written as a defence against the previous review rather than as a specification. The matched-request-axis-damage argument is restated in five separate places, the no-ratio reporting rule in four, and the 2607.14147 concession in three; meanwhile the quantities a downstream planner actually needs -- the threshold values, the layer band, the position window, the item count, the direction-fit corpus -- are either absent or buried in prose. An implementer reading this would have to reconstruct the experiment table from scratch.\",", "+      \"improvements\": [", "+        \"Add two compact tables and cut the prose that duplicates them: (1) an ESTIMAND table with one row per term (O, CB, A, coherence-net A, CB_post DiD) giving its contrast formula, its readout, its aggregation and its units; (2) a CRITERIA table with one row per pass/fail test giving the checkpoint it is registered on, the threshold value, the interval type and the MDE at the planned item count. Every threshold currently written as 'fixed before the run' becomes a number in column three.\",", "+        \"Compress each repeated argument to one statement plus a cross-reference. The matched-damage rationale, the no-ratio rule and the 2607.14147 concession each need to appear once; the duplicates cost roughly a third of the document and displace the specification content.\"", "+      ]", "+    },", "+    {", "+      \"dimension\": \"contribution\",", "+      \"score\": 3,", "+      \"justification\": \"The arming interaction is a real and, as far as I can verify, unclaimed estimand, and the methodological point behind it -- that a response-site effect measured only under harmful requests is an effect at one level of an unvaried modifier and therefore not a per-model property -- is a genuine finding-shaped claim rather than a measurement applied to more cases. The double dissociation, if it holds, would establish two separately real terms in a way no single safety scalar can produce, and the forecast direction (pre-edit arming fraction predicts post-edit survival) is a real prediction rather than a fit. The ceiling on the contribution is that the response-site pathway itself is conceded to arXiv:2607.14147 and the practical advice is conceded to arXiv:2505.19056, so what remains is one interaction term, its per-checkpoint reduction, and the safe-completion and edited arms. That is a solid workshop-to-main-track delta, not a field-moving one, and whether it lands depends entirely on whether A is measurable above the null band at the planned item count -- which the design does not yet demonstrate.\",", "+      \"improvements\": [", "+        \"Make the methodological claim the headline rather than the mechanism claim. 'A response-site safety effect reported without varying the request is an average over an unvaried modifier' is the part that changes what other people do, and it is true and publishable even if SafeRL and Qwen3-4B turn out to have the same profile. State it as the primary contribution and the per-checkpoint mechanism as the instantiation.\",", "+        \"Commit to releasing the 96 safety cells, the 96 coherence cells, the fitted directions and the per-item four-cell scalars. The artefact is what makes the arming coordinate reusable by others, and the nearest existing artefact (DrExe/qwen3-safety-vectors) is gated and has no counterfactual factor, so an ungated crossed one is an independent contribution.\"", "+      ]", "+    }", "+  ],", "+  \"critiques\": [", "+    {", "+      \"category\": \"methodology\",", "+      \"severity\": \"major\",", "+      \"description\": \"THE PRIMARY READOUT DIRECTION MAY BE THE ABLITERATION DIRECTION, WHICH WOULD MAKE THE ENTIRE STAGE-2 HEADLINE VACUOUS BY CONSTRUCTION. The hypothesis specifies the primary readout as 'projection onto the model's own harm axis, fitted by difference in means and CROSS-FITTED' over '64 short prompts to fit the readout direction' -- but it never says what the label contrast is or at which TOKEN POSITIONS the means are taken. If that direction is a difference of means over harmful versus harmless REQUESTS at prompt positions, it is the same object, produced by the same recipe, as the abliteration direction the hypothesis itself defines two paragraphs later. Abliteration orthogonalises that direction out of every matrix that writes to the residual stream, so the residual-stream component along it is driven toward zero at ALL positions, prompt and response alike. Under the PARENT-FIXED convention -- which the hypothesis pre-registers as primary for exactly this arm -- O, CB and A would then all collapse into the null band after the edit, not because the model lost a monitor but because the readout coordinate was deleted from the write path. Criterion (ii) ('CB remains above the null band after the edit in every lineage where it was above it before') would fail mechanically, criterion (i) ('A falls into the null band') would pass mechanically, and criterion (iii) would be a comparison of two near-zero residues. The hypothesis anticipates the BASIS question (it publishes cos(r_parent, r_child) and argues that refitting would change coordinates) but not the ANNIHILATION question, which is the one that decides whether Stage 2 measures anything. (2) Pre-register cos(r_readout_parent, r_ablit) as a Stage-2 gate with a stated threshold (e.g. |cos| > 0.5) above which the parent-fixed post-edit arm is declared confounded by construction and the arm is reported only under the per-checkpoint refit and the full-probe readouts, with the parent-fixed numbers shown as a labelled artefact. (3) Add a one-line component-preservation diagnostic to Stage 2: measure the variance of the edited model's residual stream along r_readout against the variance along 20 matched-norm random directions, at the same layers and positions. But P2 already predicts CB(SafeRL) >> CB(Qwen3-4B) BEFORE any edit, and it is pre-registered as an ordering criterion in its own right ('CB(SafeRL) - CB(Qwen3-4B) > delta_2'). Part (ii) then asserts that the edit leaves CB above the null band in both lineages. If both hold, CB_post(SafeRL) > CB_post(Qwen3-4B) follows from the pre-edit gap alone; the edit could damage CB equally in both lineages, or damage it MORE in SafeRL, and (iii) would still pass. So the test that is designated as load-bearing is confounded with the level difference it is supposed to be independent of, and the sentence 'the pre-edit arming fraction of each lineage is recorded BEFORE the edit so that post-edit survival is a forecast rather than a fit' is not delivered by any registered criterion. Keep the current level comparison as a labelled descriptive secondary. Additionally pre-register the forecast form explicitly: rank the two lineages by pre-edit A (or by the pre-edit arming fraction where it is defined) and state that the lineage with the SMALLER pre-edit A must show the SMALLER absolute drop in CB -- that is the statement that makes the pre-edit measurement a forecast, and it is currently only asserted in prose.\"", "+    },", "+    {", "+      \"category\": \"rigor\",", "+      \"severity\": \"major\",", "+      \"description\": \"'ABSOLUTE INTERNAL UNITS' ARE NOT COMMENSURABLE ACROSS CHECKPOINTS, AND THE DESIGN NOW ASSERTS FOUR TIMES THAT THEY ARE. That reasoning is valid for comparing two different terms WITHIN one checkpoint. It is not valid ACROSS checkpoints. A projection onto a unit direction is measured in residual-stream units, and residual-stream norms, LayerNorm gains and per-layer activation scales differ systematically between a base model, an instruction-tuned model, an RL-tuned model and a weight-edited model -- and abliteration changes them again by removing a rank-one component from every writing matrix. Every cross-checkpoint claim in the design inherits this: the safety-specificity GATE (Qwen3-4B's A must exceed Base's A 'by a margin fixed in advance, in absolute internal units'), the ordering criteria A(Qwen3-4B) - A(SafeRL) and CB(SafeRL) - CB(Qwen3-4B), and the CB_post comparison. A checkpoint with a 20% larger residual norm will show 20% larger O, CB and A with no difference in mechanism. This is an over-correction from my previous round: I pushed the design off ratios between two different main effects, and it has landed on raw un-normalised levels, which for cross-model comparison is the worse of the two errors.\",", "+      \"suggested_action\": \"Adopt a third option the design already computes and does not use. Add a mandatory descriptive table of per-checkpoint mean residual-stream norm and LayerNorm scale at the frozen band and positions, so a reader can see how large the scale differences actually are; if they are negligible the raw comparisons are vindicated cheaply, and if they are not, the design has already protected itself.\"", "+    },", "+    {", "+      \"category\": \"rigor\",", "+      \"severity\": \"major\",", "+      \"description\": \"THE PRE-REGISTRATION CONTAINS NO NUMBERS.
